# Text-only audit pre-run, 2026-10-01 (stream A)

Board task `OPUS-CLOUD-AUDIT-PRERUN-1001`. Brief: `docs/cloud-briefs/audit-prerun.md`. Report only: nothing in the snapshot was edited, and no finding below is judged; OPUS and the owners verify each one.

**PARTIAL REPORT: 140 of 159 checkers written so far.** The run continues.

How the checkers were run is under [Environment](#environment) at the end; read it before treating a CRASH as a defect in the checker, because the snapshot's layout differs from the workstation's.

Outcomes: CLEAN 24, FINDINGS 56, NEEDS-EXE 31, NEEDS-PACKAGE 22, CRASH 7.

## Findings, by checker

Each block quotes the checker's own output. Lines are grouped under the database folder they name; lines naming no page (headers, totals, the checker's own caveats) are kept under *checker summary*. Where a checker prints a line number it is in the quoted text; where it does not, the finding has none.

```text
  A type with MANY misaligned rows is most likely a packed/serialised record, which
  owes alignment nothing. A type with ONE is the interesting case: either the offset
  is wrong, or that row's size is.
  offset mod alignment - a flat spread means error, a spike means convention:
    align 2, offset % 2 == 1 : 9 row(s)
    align 4, offset % 4 == 2 : 3 row(s)
    align 4, offset % 4 == 3 : 1 row(s)
    align 8, offset % 8 == 4 : 25 row(s)
  of the 38, rows whose NAME says nobody has read them (unobserved*/unknown*/pad*) : 0
  Those carry a width that is a SPAN LENGTH, not a proven scalar, so alignment says
  nothing about them - `u64` there means eight unread bytes, not a qword.
  Remaining rows with a semantic name : 38
=== 2. tail padding: a type's size is a multiple of its strictest member alignment ===
  types with a known size and at least one testable member : 702
  size NOT a multiple of the strictest member alignment    : 9
```

```text
  matches and NOT ONE of them rests on a census:

      - 'it rules out a direct, statically visible reference in either direction' -
      and names the constructor it read rather than inferring from absence;
    * battle-record-kind-byte.md's '+0xE68 is not written' is scoped to the kind-1
      path and cites the reset that leaves 0 (00236AC2);
    * the remaining match is a working note in an agent inbox, not a publication.
  So nothing is retracted. What follows is that these censuses must be completed
  BEFORE such a claim is made, not that an existing one is wrong. Re-run
  check_nowriter_claims.py to re-test that - and the gate runner now runs it every
  suite, so the clean result is re-established rather than remembered. It was named
```

```text
UNLINKED - section 13 cites a real record that does not name this contract back (18):
  Not a false claim: the contract is citing evidence that exists.
  AND USUALLY NOT A STALE RECORD EITHER. The `contract:` key means WHAT THIS
  EXPERIMENT WAS DESIGNED TO TEST, not what may later cite it. EXP-005 and EXP-006
  carry `contract: none - a harness-capability question`, which is accurate: they
  were not run against a contract. CONTRACT-binding-profiles citing the live pointer
  chain they resolved is also accurate. **Both sides are right**, and evidence
  produced without the contract in view is MORE independent, not less.
  So the remedy is not to rewrite a correct `contract: none` into a link - that
  would make the record say something false about its own design. A record whose
  key is EMPTY is a bookkeeping gap; one that says `none` WITH A REASON is a
  declaration, and this row is then a cross-reference the corpus simply does not
  index in that direction. (Earlier text here said 'fix it there and this line
  clears', which would have had the record edited to suit the checker.)

  `contract:` frontmatter and it becomes checkable.
```

```text
      EXP-100's frontmatter reads `contract: ../10-Status-Periodic-Terminal/CONTRACT-auto-status-rebuild.md`, and that is CORRECT - it was designed to test the auto-status gate chain.   15-Input/Binding-Profiles/CONTRACT-binding-profiles.md     EXP-005, EXP-006
```
```text
      EXP-005 and EXP-006 are HARNESS-CAPABILITY questions and say so - 'can the game be driven by writing the input state it reads?' and 'measures the key-to-index mapping and the harness's reach, not a contract claim'. Answering the first required resolving the manager -> backend -> device-block -> byte-array chain IN A LIVE PROCESS, and that chain IS this contract's read path; the second measured the key-to-index mapping, which is this contract's subject. Designed for one thing, evidence for another - and evidence produced without the contract in view is MORE independent, not less. Rewriting either `contract: none` into a link would make the record say something false about its own design. Adjudicated 2026-09-14 (OPUS) from the records themselves, closing OPUS-REQ-ASTRA-EXP005-006-CONTRACT-KEY, which asked ASTRA to choose between two answers when the right answer is that both sides are already correct.
```

### `check_counts.py`: FINDINGS 1363

Command (cwd `/home/user`): `check_counts.py --db Database/FFXII TZA --limit 100000`

The checker calls each pair *worth a human's eye, not a defect*. Every pair gives the page, the table's line and the line whose number disagrees.

The output is 2985 lines, so it is filed in full at [`2026-10-01-prerun/check_counts.py.txt`](2026-10-01-prerun/check_counts.py.txt). Quoted here: the checker's own section headings and totals (lines it prints at column 0).

```text
documents scanned : 3047
tables found      : 9919
count/table pairs that disagree : 1363  (advisory -- a number near a table need not be its length)
A finding is a pair worth a human's eye, not a defect. The tool cannot tell whether the
number was ABOUT the table; it guarantees only that where a count introduces an
enumeration, the arithmetic on the page agrees with itself.
```

```text
        +0x0088 52     gambitBlockParty           GambitConfig

  BattleMenuChildWidget1620          1 row(s) below +0xC0, e.g. [('0x0', 'base0000')]

the child's declaration. A NAMED row is the serious case - two types then assert
incompatible things about the same bytes.
```

### `check_float_typed_as_int.py`: FINDINGS 49

Command (cwd `/home/user`): `check_float_typed_as_int.py`

Findings are `_types/fields.tsv` rows (type, offset, name); no file lines.

*checker summary*

```text
--- control (printed first, because everything below is a property of the classifier) ---
  fields already declared float whose accesses are float-class: 452
  censused types compared against the store: 240
--- declared-integral 4-byte fields whose accesses are EXCLUSIVELY float-class: 50 ---
  FLOAT ARITHMETIC, not a move - stronger evidence about the VALUE:
    `addss xmm,[mem]` adds the loaded dword AS a float; a move only relocates bits.
    BUT IT SETTLES THE VALUE, NOT THE OFFSET - corrected 2026-09-14 (OPUS), because the
    line that stood here said arithmetic was "stronger than the hold policy was written
    for" and named the mis-attributed base as the thing it defeats. It does not.
    The two-access rule guards against an instruction being ATTRIBUTED to the wrong
    displacement through an inferred base; whether that instruction is an add or a move
    is irrelevant to which field it touched. MEASURED in the MovementContext census:
    SEVEN sites are each attributed to 4-5 different displacements at stride 0x10 -
    003DA7C1 to 0x30/0x40/0x50/0x60/0x70, 003DA7CD to 0x34/0x44/..., 003DA73E to
    0x38/0x48/..., and 003DA60F - the ARITHMETIC one - to 0x3C/0x4C/0x6C/0x7C.
    So the "1 float access" at +0x3C and at +0x4C is the SAME INSTRUCTION seen through
    two different inferred bases, not two independent observations. Do not retype on
    this alone. Routed as OPUS-REQ-SWE2-STRUCT-MOVCTX-STRIDED-ATTRIBUTION.
    THE FAMILY REACHES INTO THE LIST BELOW TOO, so read the two lists together:
    MovementContext +0x74 and +0x78 are also stride-family rows - their float access is
    003DA7CD and 003DA73E through arg0+0x70 - and +0x74 additionally has two DIRECT but
    uninformative `mov dword ptr [rax+0x74], edi` stores. MovementContext +0x0D4 is NOT
    in the family: 002E521F is `movss dword ptr [rdi + 0xd4], xmm0`, a direct write at
    its own literal displacement, so it is an ordinary single-access hold and the only
    one of the three the two-access rule was actually written for.

  none. Reported even when empty, because an empty half of a symmetric test is a result
  and a missing half is a blind spot - which is what the first two versions of this file had.
  mixed (float AND integer accesses at the same displacement): 1 - NOT reported as defects.
  `mov dword [x], ecx` with ecx zeroed stores a float zero, so a mixed displacement is
  evidence of nothing on its own. Three lanes of the matrix at CharacterRenderRecord+0x180
  are written exactly that way.
```

```text
Overlap is often legitimate - an interior region does live inside its container -
but the store says so with a ctype naming the inner type. Two types describing one
object with neither mentioning the other is what a reader trips over.
```

### `check_route_ctype_agreement.py`: FINDINGS 2

Command (cwd `/home/user`): `check_route_ctype_agreement.py`

Findings are `_types/fields.tsv` rows; no file lines.

*checker summary*

```text
CHAIN/EMBED routes crossed against the parent's field ctype
declared types: 827   parent offsets claimed by a route: 133
ANONYMOUS ctype : 2
    ActorStorageRecord               +0xA8    CHAIN -> ActorWaitSlot                  slotsA8 / ctype='void*'
    LegacyPs2GsTextureContext        +0x150   CHAIN -> LegacyPs2GsSourceTextureRecord sourceTextureContainer / ctype='void*'
ctype spells a NON-TYPE where one is declared : 0
ctype names a DIFFERENT type : 0
no field row at that offset : 0
parent offsets claimed by MORE THAN ONE child : 0
A route is the store asserting an edge in a machine-readable column. Where the
ctype does not carry it, the claim is present and unusable. NOTHING IS WRITTEN
here: a union arm, a duplicate claimant and a deliberate blob are all legitimate
reasons for a ctype to stay anonymous, and only a person can tell them apart.
```

```text
  defect. An anchored-token fix removed 47 prefix artifacts from an earlier 351.
  SO THE CALIBRATION IS: on the ONE row in this list whose ground truth is established,
  the instrument produces a FALSE POSITIVE. Read the 312 candidates as a reading queue
  with a high false-positive rate, not as 312 defects. The one real case it was built
  from was found by reading the page, and confirming it took reading the image.
```

## Timed-out checkers

Killed at 600 s. What they printed before that is quoted for context; it is not a finding count.

### `audit_protocol_readiness.py`

```text

  passes with a named tool: 15 of 30

!! pass 22  **Unaccounted neighbours**   audit_crossrefs.py CRASHES: FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: 'Database/Tool
   pass 29  **Index and link integrity** check_links.py runs (exit 2)
```

## Environment

- Python: `Python 3.11.15` (sandbox `python3`, standard library only; nothing installed).
- Snapshot commit: `e4d2a3ba` (`git log -1 --format=%h` on `main`, the commit this branch starts from).
- Sandbox: Linux cloud container, no executable, no extracted data, no Ghidra, no `Tools/work/`.
- **Layout.** The scripts expect the workstation layout `<root>/Database/FFXII TZA` and `<root>/Database/Tools`
  (`kit_paths.py` takes the root as four parents above `gfz-scripts`). This clone has `FFXII TZA/` and `tools/` at its
  top level instead. So that defaults resolve without editing anything, the run created, **outside the clone**,
  `/home/user/Database/FFXII TZA` and `/home/user/Database/Tools` as symlinks into the clone, and ran every checker
  from `/home/user` (`KP.ROOT` = `/home/user`). The root is therefore not a git work tree: a checker that calls `git`
  on it fails, and such failures are listed as CRASH with their message, not as findings.
- **Arguments.** A checker whose parser has `--db` got `--db "Database/FFXII TZA"`; others ran with no arguments,
  except: `audit_crossrefs.py` (its required `--docs/--functions/--edges`, pointed at the absent `work/graph` census),
  `audit_inbox_backlog.py` (`--index "Database/FFXII TZA/_index"`), `check_body_paragraph_addresses.py`
  (one page, `README.md`), and query tools that need a subject, which ran `--self-test`. Where a checker truncates its
  own listing and has a flag to print everything (`--list`, `--all`), it was re-run with that flag; the command
  quoted above each findings block is the one whose output is quoted.
- `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, 600 s timeout per checker, up to four checkers at once (so *seconds* is wall time
  under light contention). `git status` on the clone was checked after the run. **One checker writes into the
  database:** `check_interior_offsets.py` appends to `_index/candidates/interior-offset-leads.tsv` (its line 267,
  `out_path = os.path.join(a.db, "_index", "candidates", "interior-offset-leads.tsv")`); here it added 21 lines. The
  file was restored with `git checkout` and nothing of it is committed. A text-only audit pass that runs this checker
  will dirty the tree it is auditing. No other file under `FFXII TZA/` or `tools/` changed.
- Outcome rules used: NEEDS-EXE covers the executable, the extracted archive and `knowledge.sqlite`, and also the
  workstation's Ghidra-derived census files under `Tools/work/` (`graph/functions.tsv`, `graph/edges.tsv`,
  `formats/...`), which are generated from the executable and are not in the snapshot. A checker that both imports a
  missing package and reads the executable is listed under the package, the first thing it hits. A checker's
  outcome is read from its output, not its exit code: several exit 0 while printing findings, and several exit 1 on
  a clean result.
- **Git copy, for checkers that ask git.** A few checkers run `git ls-files` or similar on the root. For those, a
  second root was built in the session's scratch space: `Database/FFXII TZA` and `Database/Tools` as hardlinked
  copies of the clone, committed once into a fresh local repository (commit `972d852`, one commit, no history). A
  checker re-run there says so in its note. Anything that compares against workstation history (ancestry, dates of
  commits, other branches) still cannot be reproduced.
- Checkers that read the database root from `argv[1]` and default to the current directory were given
  `"Database/FFXII TZA"`; this is stated in their note.
- Long outputs (over 600 lines) are filed in full beside this report, in `2026-10-01-prerun/<checker>.txt`, and only
  the checker's column-0 lines (its headings and totals) are quoted inline.
- **Timing.** The first pass ran four checkers at a time, so *seconds* is wall time under that load. Two checkers
  that hit the 600 s limit under load (`audit_inbox_backlog.py`, `check_doc_claims.py`) were re-run with only each
  other running; their seconds are from that re-run. `audit_protocol_readiness.py` runs the protocol's tools one
  after another, including the slow ones, and was left at its timeout.
- A child process left behind by the timed-out `audit_protocol_readiness.py` was killed before the re-runs.
- Python wrote a `__pycache__/` under `tools/analysis-kit/gfz-scripts/` despite `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` (which checker
  wrote it was not traced); it is untracked, was deleted, and is not committed.
